I’ll start cleaning out closets, then I’m rewriting the care checklists like they’re all wrong, then I’m thinking, “We should do a health fair at church,” and I’ll start calling people before breakfast.\n\nAnd I know that sounds like a lot. My sister says nobody wants a phone call that early, but in my head it feels important, like if I don’t do it right away it’ll get missed. Then one thing turns into another. Like the closet thing, I found old batteries and then I was thinking about emergency preparedness, and that made me think about the church tables—sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked. But yeah, high-energy days, I get going like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt your mood was unusually high, overly irritable, or you were filled with more energy than usual — not just being busy, but feeling different in a noticeable way, like you weren’t yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are stretches like that. I’ll feel real bright, like everything’s finally clicking into place and I’ve got the energy to fix all the things people been ignoring. Like, not just busy — more like fired up, like I can see the whole picture.\n\nBut then if somebody slows me down or says, “Maybe not right now,” I get irritated fast. I snap harder than I mean to. It’s embarrassing after, because sometimes they’re just asking a normal question, and I take it like they’re blocking me. I don’t always feel like myself in those moments… more wound up, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was so high or your energy was so intense that others noticed a clear change in you — maybe saying you were acting out of the ordinary or seemed too excited or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, people have noticed. My sister especially, she’ll say, “You’re getting too worked up,” or “Why are you talking like everything’s an emergency?” And I don’t always see it right then, because to me it feels like everything is finally lining up, like I’m bright and fired up and I can get things fixed.\n\nBut if somebody slows me down or questions me, I can snap harder than I mean to. Like they’re just asking a simple thing, but I hear it like they’re trying to stop me. Then later I’m thinking, why did I get so sharp about that? So yeah… I guess it is a clear change when I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down most of the day, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, or struggled with sleep, appetite, or concentration for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I have days where I feel low or I don’t want to be bothered, but more than two weeks like you’re saying, I’m not sure I’ve kept track of it that clean.\n\nSometimes I’ll stop doing little things I usually like, but then it’s hard to tell if that’s mood or just being tired from work. Personal care work can wear you out, you know, people think it’s just helping somebody get dressed or eat, but there’s a lot of watching and remembering—like who needs what cream, who can’t have too much salt, all that. Then I get home and I don’t feel like doing anything.\n\nSo… I can’t really say yes to the two weeks part. I’d have to think on it more.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about “nearly every day” for two weeks. I know I’ve had times where I felt heavy and didn’t want to deal with people, but I don’t keep track of days like that real good.\n\nSometimes work throws me off too, because if I’m dealing with somebody else’s needs all day, by the time I get home I’m just… done. And then I’ll sit there thinking, is this me being down, or am I just worn out? So I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other episodes where your actions felt automatic or you later couldn’t fully remember what you did, even if it wasn’t related to being worked up in a meeting?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main thing is that staff meeting. I don’t know if I’d call it automatic exactly, but it kind of felt like once I started, I couldn’t get myself to stop.\n\nWe were talking about fall prevention, and I got worked up trying to show what I meant, and next thing I’m moving chairs around and acting out the whole drill with my arms waving around. Like, showing where somebody might trip, how you’d catch it, all that. I know it sounds stupid, but in my head it felt important, like if I didn’t show it right then nobody was gonna understand.\n\nPeople were telling me to sit down, calm down, but I just kept going for a bit. I remember parts of it, but not clean like a normal meeting. It’s more like flashes of everybody looking at me and me still talking.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row, especially after those high-energy or irritable periods you described?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know that I can connect it like that, like after those worked-up times. I mean, I’ve had low stretches, yeah, but counting it out as most of the day, nearly every day, two weeks… I don’t keep track like that.\n\nSometimes after I’ve been running hot or irritated, I just feel drained, like I don’t want nobody asking me for anything. But then work does that too. I’ll come home and sit there with my shoes still on, and next thing the TV’s just playing, and I’m not even watching it. But I can’t say for sure it was hopeless for two weeks in a row.\n\nI guess I’d have to pay more attention to the days. Right now I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall a time when, after one of those high-energy or irritable periods, you felt down or hopeless for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one clear time, but I can’t really pin it down like that. Like after I’ve been all worked up, I do crash some, but I don’t know if it was most of the day every day for two weeks.\n\nSometimes the days run together, especially if I’m working a lot. One bad week feels like forever, then I look back and I don’t know if it was three days or ten. And then there’s stuff like bills, or somebody calling me needing a ride, and that can make me feel low too, so I don’t know what’s from what.\n\nSo… no, I can’t recall a definite time like that. Not with the two weeks and nearly every day part.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least a week where you felt unusually high, irritable, or so energetic that it was clearly different from your usual self, even if others might not have noticed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t always count the days right, but there are stretches where I feel real bright, like fired up, like everything is finally lining up and I can see what needs to happen.\n\nAnd then if somebody slows me down or starts questioning me, I get irritated fast. Like, harder than I mean to. It’s not even always that they did something wrong, it just feels like they’re blocking the whole thing, you know? I’ll snap, and then later I’m like, why did I come at them like that?\n\nI don’t know if other people would call it different, but it feels different to me when I’m in it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt so high, irritable, or full of energy that it lasted for several days in a row—like four days or more—where you were clearly different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… several days, yeah, I think so. I get these stretches where I feel real bright, like fired up, like everything is finally lining up in my head.\n\nAnd then if somebody slows me down or questions what I’m doing, I get irritated quick. Like I’ll snap harder than I meant to, and it’s not always even about them. It’s like they’re in the way of the whole thing.\n\nI don’t always sit there counting, you know, four days exactly, because I’m going to work and dealing with people and all that, but it can feel like it keeps going for days in a row.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if any of those low or hopeless feelings happened right after one of those high-energy or irritable periods you described, like a drop after feeling really fired up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can line it up like that. Sometimes after I’ve been fired up, yeah, I get tired or kind of flat, but I can’t say for sure it’s like a “right after” thing.\n\nBecause there’s always other stuff going on too. Like work will drain me out, or somebody in the family needs something, or I’m behind on something, and then I feel low because of that. And then I start thinking, well, was I coming down from being all revved up, or was I just worn out because I had too much on me?\n\nSo… I’ve noticed drops, I guess, but I can’t really recall a clear pattern where it’s always after those periods.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when after feeling very high, irritable, or full of energy for several days, you then felt down or hopeless for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can pin it down that cleanly, like high for several days and then exactly two weeks down. I know I’ve had times where I felt worn out after being all fired up, and then I’ll get low, but I don’t really track it like that.\n\nTwo weeks is hard for me to say. Days start blending together, especially with work… like being a personal care aide, people think it’s just helping somebody out, but it wears on you, and then you go home and still got regular life stuff waiting. So sometimes I’m down and I don’t know if it’s because I crashed after being revved up or just because everything piled up.\n\nSo… I can’t really think of a clear time like that, not in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when, after feeling very high, irritable, or full of energy for several days, you then felt down or hopeless almost every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really have a clean example of that, no. Like, I know I’ve gone from feeling really up and pushed forward to feeling kind of dragged down, but “almost every day for two weeks”... I can’t honestly say I remember it that way.\n\nSometimes I’ll think it’s been a long time, and then I look back and it was maybe a few days, or I was just exhausted from work. My schedule gets mixed up too, and I’ll be thinking about one client, then another thing at home, and it all kind of runs together.\n\nSo I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I just don’t track it that exact.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when, after feeling very high, irritable, or full of energy for several days, you then felt down or hopeless almost every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I know you asked that already, and I’m trying to think of something specific, but I just don’t have it clear like that.\n\nLike, I’ve had times where I’m real wound up or irritated for a stretch, and then later I feel low or worn down, but whether that was almost every day for two whole weeks after… I can’t say. I don’t keep track of days good like that.\n\nSometimes I’m just tired from work, and then I start thinking about bills or family stuff, and then the whole week feels bad, but I don’t know if that counts as what you mean. So… no, I can’t think of a definite time like that."
